is a sampling matrix which selects
r
rows uniformly at random without replacement.

Theorem 8.4

(SRHT is a subspace embedding, Lemma 4.1 of
[
BG13
]
,
[
Tro11
]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{S}
be an
r\times d
SRHT matrix. Let
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}
. If
r=\Theta(\varepsilon^{-2}(k+\log(d/\delta))\log(k/\delta))
, then with probability at least
1-\delta
, for all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,

\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}=(1\pm\varepsilon)\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}

Using these properties of the SRHT, we show the following main lemma of this section, Lemma
8.5
, which allows us to reduce the dimension of the points
\mathbf{a}_{i}
from
d
to
t=O(k\log k+\log^{2}n)
. This reduced dimensionality will be useful for removing a
d
dependence from our subsequent discussion. For the rest of Section
8.2.1
, we focus on proving Lemma
8.5
.

Lemma 8.5

(Dimension Reduction for
\ell_{p}
Subspace Approximation)
.

Let
\mathbf{G}
be a
t\times d
SRHT matrix (Definition
8.3
). Then, there is
t=O(k\log k+\log^{2}n)
such that, with probability at least
9/10
,

\min_{\rank(\mathbf{X})\leq k}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{A}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\leq\frac{3}{2}\mathsf{OPT}_{k}.

To prove this, we need the notion of lopsided embeddings.

Definition 8.6

(Lopsided Embedding (Definition 26,
[
CW15a
]
))
.

Consider a constraint set
\mathcal{C}
and norm
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert
, and matrices
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}
and
\mathbf{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
. Suppose
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}
satisfies:

•

\left\lVert(\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B})\mathbf{S}^{\top}\right\rVert\geq(1-\varepsilon)\left\lVert\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}\right\rVert
for all
\mathbf{Y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}

•

\left\lVert(\mathbf{Y}^{*}\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B})\mathbf{S}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq(1+\varepsilon)\left\lVert\mathbf{Y}^{*}\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}\right\rVert
, where
\mathbf{Y}^{*}=\argmin_{\mathbf{Y}\in\mathcal{C}}\left\lVert\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}\right\rVert

Then,
\mathbf{S}
is a
\varepsilon
-lopsided embedding for
(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})
with respect to
\mathcal{C}
and
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert
.

The next lemma from prior work shows the utility of lopsided embeddings for subspace approximation, showing that if we can maintain a sketch
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{S}^{\top}
where
\mathbf{S}
is a lopsided embedding, then solving for the best rank
k
approximation in the column space of
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{S}^{\top}
is sufficient for obtaining a good subspace approximation solution.

Lemma 8.7

(Lemma B.1,
[
FKW21
]
,
[
CW15a
]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
and
\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}
be matrices such that

\left\lVert\mathbf{U}\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{A}\right\rVert_{p,2}=\min_{\rank(\mathbf{X})\leq k}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{X})\right\rVert_{p,2}.

If
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times d}
is a lopsided
\varepsilon
-embedding for
(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{A})
with respect to
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{p,2}
, then

\min_{\rank(\mathbf{X})\leq k}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{S}^{\top}\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{A}\right\rVert_{p,2}\leq(1+O(\varepsilon))\min_{\rank(\mathbf{X})\leq k}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{X})\right\rVert_{p,2}

The work of
[
FKW21
]
shows that a random Gaussian matrix
\mathbf{G}
with
\tilde{O}(k)
columns gives
O(1)
-lopsided embeddings for the
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{1,2}
norm, based on results from
[
CW15a
]
. We show an analogous result for the
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{p,2}
norm and for the SRHT. As done in
[
FKW21
]
, we use the sufficient conditions for a lopsided embedding provided by
[
CW15a
, Lemma 27]
.

Lemma 8.8

(Lemma 27,
[
CW15a
]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}
and
\mathbf{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
. Suppose
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times d}
satisfies the following:

•

With probability at least
1-\delta/3
,
\mathbf{S}
is a subspace
\varepsilon
-contraction for
\mathbf{A}^{\top}
, that is, simultaneously for all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,

\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}\geq(1-\varepsilon)\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}

•

For all
i\in[n]
, with probability at least
1-\delta\varepsilon^{p+1}/3
,
\mathbf{S}
is a subspace
\varepsilon^{p+1}
-contraction for
[\mathbf{A}^{\top}\ \mathbf{B}^{\top}\mathbf{e}_{i}]
, that is, simultaneously for all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k+1}
,

\left\lVert\mathbf{S}[\mathbf{A}^{\top}\ \mathbf{B}^{\top}\mathbf{e}_{i}]\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}\geq(1-\varepsilon)\left\lVert\mathbf{S}[\mathbf{A}^{\top}\ \mathbf{B}^{\top}\mathbf{e}_{i}]\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}

•

With probability at least
1-\delta/3
,

\left\lVert\mathbf{B}^{*}\mathbf{S}^{\top}\right\rVert_{p,2}\leq(1+\varepsilon^{p+1})\left\lVert\mathbf{B}^{*}\right\rVert_{p,2}

where
\mathbf{B}^{*}=\mathbf{Y}^{*}\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}
for
\mathbf{Y}^{*}=\argmin_{\mathbf{Y}}\left\lVert\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}\right\rVert_{p,2}

Then,
\mathbf{S}
is a lopsided
\varepsilon
-embedding for
(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B})
with respect to
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{p,2}
.

With the above results in hand, we can prove Lemma
8.5
.

Proof of Lemma
8.5
.

We check the conditions for Lemma
8.8
to show that
\mathbf{G}
is a lopsided embedding. In turn, we will apply Lemma
8.7
to conclude. The first two follow from the subspace embedding guarantees for SRHT in Theorem
8.4
with constant
\delta
and
\varepsilon
. For the last, we apply Theorem
8.4
with
\delta=1/\poly(n)
and
k=1
to show that the norm of each of the
n
rows of
\mathbf{B}^{*}
is preserved up to a factor of
(1\pm\varepsilon^{p+1})
, which implies the required condition.
∎

8.2.2
Online Point Reduction

Next, we use the previous dimension reduction result in combination with online Lewis weights
[
WY23
]
to obtain a small online coreset for a
O(1)
approximation. The following is Lemma B.4 of
[
FKW21
]
, whose argument applies directly to all
p\geq 1
, and states that any subspace embedding
\mathbf{L}
for the matrix
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}
which preserves the
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{p,2}
-norm of an arbitrary matrix in expectation preserves the subspace approximation cost of
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}
, up to a constant factor. We will apply this lemma with
\mathbf{L}
chosen to be sampled according to the online Lewis weights of
[
WY23
]
, which indeed satisfy the hypotheses.

Lemma 8.9

(Lemma B.4
[
FKW21
]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{G}\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times d}
be an SRHT matrix. Let
\mathbf{L}
be a random matrix such that with probability at least
9/10
, simultaneously for all
\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{t}
,

\alpha\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\mathbf{y}\right\rVert_{p}\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{L}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\mathbf{y}\right\rVert_{p}\leq\beta\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\mathbf{y}\right\rVert_{p}

and

\E_{\mathbf{L}}[\left\lVert\mathbf{L}\mathbf{M}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}]=\left\lVert\mathbf{M}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}

for any matrix
\mathbf{M}
. Then, there is
t=O(k\log k+\log^{2}n)
such that with probability at least
3/5
, all matrices
\mathbf{X}
with

\left\lVert\mathbf{L}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{A}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\leq 10\cdot\mathsf{OPT}_{k}

satisfy

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{A}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\leq(2+40/\alpha)^{p}\mathsf{OPT}_{k}.

We may now show our online point reduction lemma. While the result only holds for a fixed
i\in[n]
with constant probability, we may boost the success probability by taking
O(\log(n/\delta))
independent copies, so that we have at least one good bicriteria approximation for all
i\in[n]
with probability at least
1-\delta
. This will be enough for our uses.

We use the following online Lewis weight sampling theorem, which provides the subspace embedding
\mathbf{L}
we need in Lemma
8.9
.

Theorem 8.10

(Online Lewis Weight Sampling
[
WY22
,
WY23
]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n\times d}
with entries bounded by
\Delta
and let
p\in(0,\infty)
. Let
\delta\in(0,1)
be a failure rate parameter and let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
be an accuracy parameter. Then there is an online coreset algorithm
\mathcal{A}
such that, with probability at least
1-\delta
,
\mathcal{A}
outputs a weighted subset of
m
rows with sampling matrix
\mathbf{S}
such that

\left\lVert\mathbf{S}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}=(1\pm\varepsilon)\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}

(14)

for all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, for every
i\in[n]
, and

m=\begin{cases}O\left\lparen\frac{d^{p/2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right\rparen\log(n\Delta)^{p/2+1}\left[(\log d)^{2}(\log n)+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right]&p\in(2,\infty)\\
O\left\lparen\frac{d}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right\rparen\log(n\Delta)\left[(\log d)^{2}\log n+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right]&p\in(1,2)\\
O\left\lparen\frac{d}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right\rparen\log(n\Delta)\log\frac{n}{\delta}&p=1\\
O\left\lparen\frac{d}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right\rparen\log(n\Delta)\left[(\log d)^{3}+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right]&p\in(0,1)\end{cases}

Proof.

Theorem 3.9 of
[
WY22
]
shows that one can construct weights
\mathbf{w}_{i}\leq O(1)
for
i\in[n]
in an online fashion such that there is a fixed
s=O(n)
such that
s\cdot\mathbf{w}^{1/2-1/p}_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
i\in[n]
, and satisfies

\mathbf{w}_{i}\leq O(1)\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}_{i}^{\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{W}^{1/2-1/p}\mathbf{A})

for every
i\in[n]
, where
\mathbf{W}=\diag(\mathbf{w})
. Since
\mathbf{W}^{1/2-1/p}\mathbf{A}
is an
n\times d
integer matrix with entries bounded by
\Delta
, the proof of
[
WY22
, Theorem 1.5]
shows that the sum of the weights is at most

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{w}_{i}\leq O(1)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\tau}_{i}^{\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{W}^{1/2-1/p}\mathbf{A})\leq O(d\log(n\Delta)).

Furthermore, the weights
\mathbf{w}
constructed in
[
WY22
, Theorem 3.9]
satisfy the
one-sided Lewis weight property
(see
[
WY23
, Definition 2.2]
), which means that sampling proportionally to these weights gives the guarantee of (
14
) by
[
WY23
, Theorem 5.2]
for
p>2
or
[
WY23
, Theorem A.2]
, with the sample complexity as stated.
∎

We then obtain the following lemma, which reduces our original problem of finding a constant factor solution for the matrix
\mathbf{A}_{i}
, to solving a regression problem on a small subsample given by
\mathbf{L}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{G}^{\top}
and
\mathbf{L}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}
.

Lemma 8.11

(Online Point Reduction)
.

Let
\mathbf{G}\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times d}
be an SRHT matrix with
t=O(k\log k+\log^{2}n)
. Let
\mathbf{L}
be an
\ell_{p}
online Lewis weight sample generated by applying Theorem
8.10
to
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}
with
\varepsilon=1/2
and
\delta=9/10
. Let
\mathbf{L}_{i}
denote the online coreset
\mathbf{L}
at time
i\in[n]
. Let
\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}
satisfy

\left\lVert\mathbf{L}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}-\mathbf{L}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\leq\frac{6}{5}\min_{\mathbf{Y}\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times d}}\left\lVert\mathbf{L}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{L}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}.

Note that such a
\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}
can be found in polynomial time by converting to an instance of
\ell_{p}
regression using Dvoretzky’s theorem
[
SW18
,
FKW21
]
. Then for each fixed
i\in[n]
, with probability at least
3/10
,

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}-\mathbf{A}_{i}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\leq O(1)\min_{\rank(\mathbf{X})\leq k}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{X})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}

Proof.

Fix
i\in[n]
and let
\mathsf{OPT}_{k}
denote the optimal rank
k

\ell_{p}
subspace approximation cost for
\mathbf{A}_{i}
. Note that the column space of
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}
contains a
3/2
-approximate solution by Lemma
8.5
. Then by Markov’s inequality over the draws of
\mathbf{L}_{i}
, with probability at least
1-1/5-1/10=7/10
, there is a rank
k
projection
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}
such that

\left\lVert\mathbf{L}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}-\mathbf{L}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\leq\frac{15}{2}\cdot\mathsf{OPT}_{k}.

We may then lower bound this by minimizing over all
t\times d
matrices
\mathbf{Y}
instead of rank
k
matrices, so

\left\lVert\mathbf{L}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\mathbf{L}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\leq\frac{6}{5}\min_{\mathbf{Y}\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times d}}\left\lVert\mathbf{L}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{L}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\leq\frac{6}{5}\cdot\frac{15}{2}\cdot\mathsf{OPT}_{k}<10\cdot\mathsf{OPT}_{k},

where
\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}
is the minimizer over all matrices without the rank constraint. By a union bound with the event from Lemma
8.9
, we have that with probability at least
3/10
,

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\mathbf{A}_{i}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\leq O(1)\mathsf{OPT}_{k}.\qed

Note that
\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}
found from Lemma
8.11
may not necessarily be an integer matrix, even if
\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{G}^{\top}
is. We thus need to round
\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}
. If
\rank(\mathbf{A}_{i})\geq 2t
, then we use the next lemma, Lemma
8.12
, to carry this out. Otherwise, we just directly store a basis for
\rowspan(\mathbf{A}_{i})
.

Lemma 8.12
.

Let
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n\times t}
and
\mathbf{B}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n\times d}
be integer matrices with entries bounded by
\Delta
, and let
\mathbf{Y}\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times d}
. Suppose that
\rank(\mathbf{B})\geq 2t
. Then, rounding each entry of
\mathbf{Y}
to the nearest integer multiple of
1/\poly(n\Delta)
produces a matrix
\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}
such that

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\mathbf{B}\right\rVert_{p,2}\leq(1+\poly(n\Delta)^{-1})\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{B}\right\rVert_{p,2}

Proof.

Note that

\min_{\mathbf{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times d}}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{B}\right\rVert_{p,2}\geq\frac{1}{\poly(n)}\min_{\mathbf{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times d}}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{B}\right\rVert_{F}.

By Lemma 4.1 of
[
CW09
]
, since the rank of
\mathbf{B}
is at least
2t
, we have that

\min_{\mathbf{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times d}}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{B}\right\rVert_{F}\geq\frac{1}{\poly(n\Delta)}.

Then,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\mathbf{B}\right\rVert_{p,2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{B}\right\rVert_{p,2}+\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\mathbf{Y})\right\rVert_{p,2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{B}\right\rVert_{p,2}+\poly(n)\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\mathbf{Y})\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{B}\right\rVert_{p,2}+\poly(n)\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\right\rVert_{2}\left\lVert\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\mathbf{Y}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{B}\right\rVert_{p,2}+\poly(n\Delta)^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\poly(n\Delta)^{-1})\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{B}\right\rVert_{p,2}\qed

8.3
Online Sensitivity Approximation

In Section
8.2
, we have shown how to find a constant factor bicriteria solution in an online fashion. Using our online constant factor bicriteria solution, we now show that we can estimate sensitivities in an online manner, and that they have a small sum.

Definition 8.13

(Online Sensitivity)
.

Let
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
. Then, the
i
th online sensitivity for the rank
k

\ell_{p}
subspace approximation problem is

\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}^{\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{A})\coloneqq\sup_{F\in\mathcal{F}_{k}}\frac{\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{F})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}}{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{F})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}},

where
\mathcal{F}_{k}
is the set of rank
k
subspaces, and
\mathbf{P}_{F}
is the orthogonal projection matrix onto the subspace
F
. Equivalently,
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}^{\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{A})=\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i})
, where
\sigma_{i}
is the usual sensitivity
[
VX12
, Definition 2]

\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}(\mathbf{A})\coloneqq\sup_{F\in\mathcal{F}_{k}}\frac{\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{F})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}}{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{F})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}}.

We first adapt an argument from
[
VX12
, Theorem 7]
, which shows that it suffices to bound sensitivities over an approximately optimal bicriteria subspace.

Lemma 8.14

(Theorem 7,
[
VX12
]
)
.

Let
\tilde{F}
be a rank
r
subspace such that

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\leq\alpha\cdot\mathsf{OPT}_{k}=\alpha\min_{\rank(\mathbf{X})\leq k}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{X})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}

for some
\alpha\geq 1
. Then,

\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}(\mathbf{A})\leq 2^{2p-1}\alpha\cdot\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})+2^{p-1}\alpha\frac{\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}}{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}}.

Proof.

If
\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}=0
, then
\tilde{F}
contains
\rowspan(\mathbf{A})
so the bound holds, so assume otherwise. For any rank
k
subspace
F\in\mathcal{F}
and
i\in[n]
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{F})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}}\mathbf{P}_{F}\right\rVert_{2}^{p}

optimality of
\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{F}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}\left[\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}+\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{F})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}\right]

triangle inequality

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}\left[\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}+\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{F})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\right]

Note that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{F})\right\rVert_{p,2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{F}\right\rVert_{p,2}

optimality of
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}}\mathbf{P}_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}}-\mathbf{A}\right\rVert_{p,2}+\left\lVert\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{F}\right\rVert_{p,2}

triangle inequality

\displaystyle\leq(\alpha^{1/p}+1)\left\lVert\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{F}\right\rVert_{p,2}

near optimality of
\tilde{F}

\displaystyle\leq 2\alpha^{1/p}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{F}\right\rVert_{p,2}

Thus, we continue to bound

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{F})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}\left[\frac{\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}}{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{F}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}}+2^{p}\alpha\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right]\left\lVert\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{F}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}\left[\alpha\frac{\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}}{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}}+2^{p}\alpha\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right]\left\lVert\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{F}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}

by near optimality of
\tilde{F}
. Taking a supremum over
F\in\mathcal{F}_{k}
yields the desired result.
∎

Consider instantiating the bicriteria solution
\tilde{F}
from the above by the row span of
\mathbf{G}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, which is a rank
t
integer matrix after scaling by
\poly(n\Delta)^{t}
, and is a good solution by Lemma
8.11
. Note that
\mathbf{G}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}
only changes when we draw a new row when sampling from the online Lewis weights of
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}
, since
\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}
only depends on the rows sampled by the online Lewis weights by the construction in Lemma
8.11
. This occurs only
m
times, where
m
is the sample complexity of the online Lewis weights (see Theorem
8.10
). Then, we can partition the stream into
m
segments, and separately bound the online sensitivities of each of these
m
substreams. The advantage of this is that within each of the substreams, we only need to bound the distances to the fixed subspace
\tilde{F}=\rowspan(\mathbf{G}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{Y}})
and then online sensitivities within
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}}
. To bound the sensitivities within
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}}
, we show that the online Lewis weights of
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}}
give a good bound:

Lemma 8.15

(Lewis Weights Bound
(p,2)
-Sensitivities)
.

Let
\mathbf{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}
, and let
\mathbf{w}^{p,\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{B})
be one-sided online Lewis weights for
\mathbf{B}
. Then, for every
l\in[n]
,

\sup_{\mathbf{Y}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}}\frac{\left\lVert\mathbf{b}_{l}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}\right\rVert_{2}^{p}}{\left\lVert\mathbf{B}_{l}\mathbf{Y}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}}\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{w}^{p,\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{B})\right\rVert_{1}^{0\lor(p/2-1)}\mathbf{w}_{l}^{p,\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{B})

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
be a change of basis matrix so that
\mathbf{W}^{p,\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{B})_{l}^{1/2-1/p}\mathbf{B}_{l}\mathbf{R}
is orthonormal. By the one-sided Lewis property of online Lewis weights (Lemma 3.6 of
[
WY23
]
), we have that

\left\lVert\mathbf{b}_{l}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{Y}\right\rVert_{2}^{p}\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{b}_{l}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\right\rVert_{2}^{p}\left\lVert\mathbf{Y}\right\rVert_{2}^{p}\leq\mathbf{w}_{l}^{p,\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{B})\left\lVert\mathbf{Y}\right\rVert_{2}^{p}.

We then bound

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{Y}\right\rVert_{2}^{p}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\left\lVert\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}=1}\left\lVert\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}^{p}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\left\lVert\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}=1}\left\lVert\mathbf{W}^{p,\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{B})_{l}^{1/2-1/p}\mathbf{B}_{l}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\left\lVert\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}=1}\left\lVert\mathbf{w}^{p,\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{B})_{l}\right\rVert_{1}^{0\lor(p/2-1)}\left\lVert\mathbf{B}_{l}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}

Lemma 2.3 of
[
WY23
]

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\mathbf{w}^{p,\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{B})_{l}\right\rVert_{1}^{0\lor(p/2-1)}\sup_{\left\lVert\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}=1}\sum_{i=1}^{l}\left\lvert\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{B}_{l}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{x}\right\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{w}^{p,\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{B})_{l}\right\rVert_{1}^{0\lor(p/2-1)}\sum_{i=1}^{l}\sup_{\left\lVert\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}=1}\left\lvert\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{B}_{l}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{x}\right\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\mathbf{w}^{p,\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{B})_{l}\right\rVert_{1}^{0\lor(p/2-1)}\sum_{i=1}^{l}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{B}_{l}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{Y}\right\rVert_{2}^{p}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\mathbf{w}^{p,\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{B})_{l}\right\rVert_{1}^{0\lor(p/2-1)}\left\lVert\mathbf{B}_{l}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{Y}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}.

Chaining these bounds together yields the claimed result.
∎

We can now apply Lemma
8.15
with
\mathbf{B}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}}=\mathbf{A}(\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\mathbf{G})(\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\mathbf{G})^{-}
. In fact, we can apply Lemma
8.15
with
\mathbf{B}=\mathbf{A}\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\mathbf{G}
, since the online Lewis weights only depend on the column span of the matrix. By Lemma
8.12
, this is an
n\times t
integer matrix with entries bounded by
\poly(n\Delta)
up to scaling, unless
\rank(\mathbf{A})\leq 2t
, in which case we can just use the online Lewis weights of
\mathbf{A}
directly, as we will show. This gives us the following algorithm, Algorithm
3
, for approximating online sensitivities:

Algorithm 3

Online Sensitivity Approximation

input:

\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n\times d}
with entries bounded by
\Delta
, rank
k
.

output:
Online sensitivity approximations
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}^{\mathsf{OL}}
.

1:

Draw a
t\times d
SRHT matrix
\mathbf{G}
for
t=O(k\log k+\log^{2}n)

2:

for

i\in[n]

do

3:

\mathbf{L}_{i}=\textsc{OnlineLewis}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{G}^{\top})

4:

if

\mathbf{L}_{i}
sampled a new row

then

5:

Solve for
\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}
as in Lemma
8.11
and rounded as in Lemma
8.12

6:

\tilde{F}_{i}\leftarrow\rowspan(\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i})

7:

v_{i}\leftarrow\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}_{i}})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}

8:

else

9:

\tilde{F}_{i}\leftarrow\tilde{F}_{i-1}
,
\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}\leftarrow\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i-1}

10:

v_{i}\leftarrow v_{i}+\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}_{i}})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}

11:

\tilde{\mathbf{w}}_{i}^{\mathsf{OL}}\leftarrow\textsc{OnlineLewis}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{G})

12:

\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}^{\mathsf{OL}}\leftarrow O(1)\left[\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}_{i}})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}/v_{i}+O(t\log(n\Delta))^{0\lor(p/2-1)}\tilde{\mathbf{w}}_{i}^{\mathsf{OL}}\right]

Lemma 8.16

(Sum of Online Sensitivities)
.

Let
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n\times d}
have entries bounded by
\Delta
. Fix
i\in[n]
. Then, with probability at least
3/10
, Algorithm
3
returns an upper bound
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}^{\mathsf{OL}}
on the online sensitivity such that

\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}^{\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{A})\leq\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}^{\mathsf{OL}}.

Furthermore,

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}^{\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{A})\leq O(t^{2}\log^{2}(n\Delta))^{1\lor(p/2)}(\log^{2}t)(\log n)

where
t=O(k\log k+\log^{2}n)
.

Proof.

Fix
i\in[n]
. Then, by Lemma
8.11
, there is a
3/10
probability that
\mathbf{L}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}
spans an
O(1)
-approximately optimal solution. Condition on this event and let
\tilde{F}_{i}=\rowspan(\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{i})
. Then by Lemma
8.14
, we may bound the
i
th online sensitivity by

\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}^{\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{A})=\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i})\leq O(1)\left[\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})+\frac{\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}}{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}}\right].

Now let
\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}
denote the last
I
rows of
\mathbf{A}_{i}
, where
I
is the number of rows that have streamed in since the last time a row was sampled from
\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{G}^{\top}
in Line
3
. That is, it is the set of rows which used the same subspace
\tilde{F}_{i}
as row
i
. Note then that

\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}^{\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{A})\leq O(1)\left[\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})+\frac{\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\prime\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}}{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}}\right].

By Lemma
8.15
and the bit complexity bound from Lemma
8.12
, the first term can be bounded by the online Lewis weight, i.e.,

\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\leq O(t\log(n\Delta))^{0\lor(p/2-1)}\mathbf{w}_{i}^{p,\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}}).

We now bound the sum. First fix a segment of the stream which has the same
\tilde{F}_{i}
. Note that there are at most
m=O(t\log(n\Delta))^{1\lor(p/2)}(\log^{2}t)(\log n)
such segments by Theorem
8.10
. Similarly, the sum of online Lewis weights in this segment is bounded by
O(t\log(n\Delta))^{1\lor(p/2)}
by Lemma 3.7 of
[
WY23
]
. This gives a total contribution of

O(t\log(n\Delta))^{1\lor(p/2)}\cdot O(t\log(n\Delta))^{1\lor(p/2)}(\log^{2}k)(\log n)=O(t^{2}\log^{2}(n\Delta))^{1\lor(p/2)}(\log^{2}t)(\log n)

To bound the second term, we bound the number of times which
\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}
can double as
i
ranges over
[n]
. Note that

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\geq\min_{\rank(\mathbf{X})\leq t}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{X})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\geq\frac{1}{\poly(n)}\min_{\rank(\mathbf{X})\leq t}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{X})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}=\sigma_{t+1}(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime})^{p}\geq\poly(n\Delta)^{-t}

We also have

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\leq\poly(n\Delta)\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{2}^{p}.

Thus,
\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}
can double at most
O(t\log(n\Delta))
times. Then in each of these windows in which the the mass
\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}
does not double, the
\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\prime\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}
must sum to
O(\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\prime}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\tilde{F}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p})
so the second term adds up to at most
O(t\log(n\Delta))
in each segment, which is dominated by the sum of online Lewis weights.
∎

Remark 8.17
.

If we do not need to algorithmically approximate the online sensitivities, then we can get better existential bounds. Indeed, the argument in Section
1.2.2
shows a bound of

O(k\log(n\Delta))^{1+(1\lor(p/2))},

for integer inputs, or

O(k\log(n\kappa^{\mathsf{OL}}))^{1\lor(p/2)}\log(n\kappa^{\mathsf{OL}}),

for real inputs. For random order streams, an even simpler argument of
[
CMP20
]
yields a bound of

O(k^{1\lor(p/2)}\log n).

Indeed, we can view the online sensitivity of row
i
as the sensitivity of a random row among a subset of
i
random rows, which is at most
s/i
in expectation, where
s=O(k^{1\lor(p/2)})
is an upper bound on the offline total sensitivity, using Lemma
8.15
. By linearity of expectation, this is at most
O(s\log n)
in expectation.

8.4
Sensitivity Sampling

With sensitivity estimates in hand, we now use sensitivity sampling results for subspace approximation from
[
HV20
]
.

To show sensitivity sampling bounds that are independent of
d
,
[
HV20
]
use the result of
[
SW18
]
which states that there exists a
O(k/\varepsilon^{2})
-dimensional subspace
\Gamma
which preserves subspace approximation objectives, which then implies that it is sufficient to prove coreset guarantees over the low dimensional subspace
\Gamma
instead.

We will need the following results of
[
SW18
]
, with the theorem numbering from arXiv version 2.

Lemma 8.18

(Lemma 6,
[
SW18
]
)
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
and let
\tau=\varepsilon^{1\lor(2/p)}
. Let
W
be a subspace of dimension at most
k
. If
V
is a subspace of any dimension such that

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{V})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\leq(1+\varepsilon)\mathsf{OPT}_{k}

and

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{V})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{V\cup W})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\leq\tau\mathsf{OPT}_{k},

then

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{V}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{V\cup W}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\leq O(\varepsilon)\mathsf{OPT}_{k}.

Such a subspace
V
can be constructed by Algorithm 1 of
[
SW18
]
.

Lemma 8.19

(Theorem 10,
[
SW18
]
)
.

Let
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a subspace such that for all rank
k
subspaces
W\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{V}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{V\cup W}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\leq\varepsilon^{p+3}\cdot\mathsf{OPT}_{k}.

Let
\mathbf{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times(d+1)}
be the matrix with
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{V}
in its first
d
columns and
\{\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{V})\right\rVert_{2}\}_{i=1}^{n}
as its
(d+1)
st column. Then for all rank
k
subspaces
W\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\mathbf{P}_{W})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}=(1\pm O(\varepsilon))\left\lVert\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{I}_{d+1}-\mathbf{P}_{W}^{\prime})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p},

where
\mathbf{P}_{W}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{(d+1)\times(d+1)}
is the projection matrix which applies
\mathbf{P}_{W}
on the first
d
coordinates and zeros out the
(d+1)
st coordinate.

We will adapt Lemma 5.7 of
[
HV20
]
to the
\ell_{p}
subspace approximation problem. We will need their Theorem 5.10, with a couple of adjustments: their use of results from
[
FL11
]
are replaced by the corresponding “independent sampling” version of Theorem
B.9
, and their use of the existence of a set of
\tilde{O}(k^{2}/\varepsilon)
points
[
SV07
,
SV12
]
spanning a
(1+\varepsilon)
-optimal solution is replaced by the strong coresets of
[
SW18
]
of size
O(k^{1\lor(p/2)}/\varepsilon^{(p+3)(1\lor(2/p))})
, which has a better dependence on
k
for
p<4
.

Lemma 8.20

(Weak Coreset for Subspace Approximation, Theorem 5.10,
[
HV20
]
)
.

Suppose
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}(\mathbf{A})
satisfies

\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}(\mathbf{A})\geq\sup_{F\in\mathcal{F}_{k}}\frac{\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{F})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}}{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{F})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}}

Let
\tilde{\mathfrak{S}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}(\mathbf{A})
denote the total sensitivity and let
\mathcal{S}\geq\tilde{\mathfrak{S}}
be an upper bound on the total sensitivity. Suppose a sampling matrix
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{s\times n}
is constructed as done in Theorem
B.9
, with VC-dimension upper bound

d=O(k)\cdot\min\left\{\varepsilon^{-1}k^{2}\log(k/\varepsilon),\varepsilon^{-(p+3)(1\lor(2/p))}k^{1\lor(p/2)}\right\}

Then with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have that

\min_{F\in\mathcal{F}_{k}}\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{F})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}=(1\pm\varepsilon)\min_{F\in\mathcal{F}_{k}}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{F})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}

and
\mathbf{S}
samples at most

O\left\lparen\frac{\mathcal{S}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\lparen dk\log\mathcal{S}+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right\rparen\right\rparen

rows.

The above results can be used to show a version of Lemmas
8.18
and
8.19
for the sampled matrix
\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}
. This simplifies and sharpens Lemma 5.7 of
[
HV20
]
for
\ell_{p}
subspace approximation.

Lemma 8.21
.

Let
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\varepsilon^{(p+3)\cdot(1\lor(2/p))}
. Let
\mathbf{S}
be sampled as in Lemma
8.20
, using rank
2k
sensitivities, where the expected number of rows sampled is

s=O\left\lparen\mathcal{S}\left\lparen k\cdot\min\{k^{2}\log k,k^{1\lor(p/2)}\}\log\mathcal{S}+\varepsilon^{\prime-2}\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right\rparen\right\rparen

Suppose
\Gamma\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a subspace such that for any subspace
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
of dimension at most
k
,

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma\cup W})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{OPT}_{k}(\mathbf{A}).

(15)

and also contains
V^{*}
, where

V^{*}=\arg\min_{V^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{k}}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{V^{\prime}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}.

Let
\mathbf{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times(d+1)}
be the matrix with
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}
in its first
d
columns and
\{\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma})\right\rVert_{2}\}_{i=1}^{n}
as its
(d+1)
st column. Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
, for any rank
k
subspace
W
,

\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\mathbf{P}_{W})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}=(1\pm O(\varepsilon))\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{I}_{d+1}-\mathbf{P}_{W}^{\prime})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p},

where
\mathbf{P}_{W}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{(d+1)\times(d+1)}
is the projection matrix which applies
\mathbf{P}_{W}
on the first
d
coordinates and zeros out the
(d+1)
st coordinate.

Proof.

We will show that (
15
) implies a similar condition for
\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}
, which yields the result by Lemma
8.19
. Thus, we will bound

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma\cup W})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}-\min_{W^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{k}}\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma\cup W^{\prime}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma\cup W^{*}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}

where

W^{*}=\arg\min_{W^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{k}}\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma\cup W^{\prime}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}.

Recall that
V^{*}
is the optimal rank
k
solution for
\mathbf{A}
achieving the value
\mathsf{OPT}_{k}(\mathbf{A})
. Note that

\frac{1}{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}(\mathbf{A})}\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma\cup W^{*}})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}(\mathbf{A})}\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{V^{*}\cup W^{*}})\right\rVert_{2}^{p}\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{V^{*}\cup W^{*}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\leq\mathsf{OPT}_{k}(\mathbf{A})

since
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}(\mathbf{A})
upper bound the rank
2k
sensitivities. Then by Bernstein’s inequality, we have that

\Pr\left\{\left\lvert\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma\cup W^{*}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma\cup W^{*}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\right\rvert\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}\mathsf{OPT}_{k}(\mathbf{A})\right\}\leq 2\exp\left\lparen-\frac{s}{\mathcal{S}}\frac{(\varepsilon^{\prime}\mathsf{OPT}_{k}(\mathbf{A}))^{2}}{2(1+\varepsilon^{\prime}/3)\mathsf{OPT}_{k}(\mathbf{A})^{2}}\right\rparen<\delta

since
s\geq C\mathcal{S}\varepsilon^{\prime-2}\log(1/\delta)
for a large enough constant
C
. Similarly,

\Pr\left\{\left\lvert\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\right\rvert\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}\mathsf{OPT}_{k}(\mathbf{A})\right\}\leq 2\exp\left\lparen-\frac{s}{\mathcal{S}}\frac{(\varepsilon^{\prime}\mathsf{OPT}_{k}(\mathbf{A}))^{2}}{2(1+\varepsilon^{\prime}/3)\mathsf{OPT}_{k}(\mathbf{A})^{2}}\right\rparen<\delta.

Thus, conditioned on the success of these events, we have that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma\cup W^{*}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma\cup W^{*}})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}+2\varepsilon^{\prime}\mathsf{OPT}_{k}(\mathbf{A})

\displaystyle\leq 3\varepsilon^{\prime}\mathsf{OPT}_{k}(\mathbf{A}).

Since
\mathbf{S}
preserves the optimal cost up to
O(1)
factors by Lemma
8.20
, this is bounded by
O(\varepsilon^{\prime})\mathsf{OPT}_{k}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A})
. Finally, we have by Lemma
8.18
that

\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{\Gamma\cup W}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}\leq\varepsilon^{p+3}\cdot\mathsf{OPT}_{k}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A})

for all rank
k
subspaces
W
, and by Lemma
8.19
that

\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\mathbf{P}_{W})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}=(1\pm O(\varepsilon))\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{I}_{d+1}-\mathbf{P}_{W}^{\prime})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}

for all rank
k
subspaces
W
.
∎

By Lemma
8.21
, it now suffices to show coreset guarantees for the low dimensional subspace spanned by the rows of
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}_{V}
, rather than
\mathbf{A}
. Indeed, if we show that

\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{I}_{d+1}-\mathbf{P}_{W}^{\prime})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}=(1\pm O(\varepsilon))\left\lVert\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{I}_{d+1}-\mathbf{P}_{W}^{\prime})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}

(16)

for all
W\in\mathcal{F}_{k}
, then this implies that

\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\mathbf{P}_{W})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}=(1\pm O(\varepsilon))\left\lVert\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\mathbf{P}_{W})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}

by the chain of approximations from Lemmas
8.21
and
8.19
, using the subspace
\Gamma
as constructed in Algorithm 1 of
[
SW18
]
. To show (
16
), it suffices to use the independent sampling version
[
FL11
]
again (Theorem
B.9
), this time using the ambient dimension as an upper bound on the VC-dimension, which is just
O(k/\varepsilon^{(p+3)\cdot(1\lor(2/p)})\times k
. This is analogous to Lemma 5.5 of
[
HV20
]
.

Lemma 8.22
.

Let
\mathbf{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
a rank
r
matrix. Let
\mathbf{S}
be sampled as in Theorem
B.9
with
d=rk
. Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
, simultaneously for all rank
k
subspaces
W
,

\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\mathbf{P}_{W})\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}=(1\pm\varepsilon)\left\lVert\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\mathbf{P}_{W}\right\rVert_{p,2}^{p}.

9
Online Coresets for Entrywise
\ell_{p}
Low Rank Approximation

We discuss how an argument of
[
JLL+21
]
, together with our Theorem
1.21
, gives the following result on online coresets for entrywise
\ell_{p}
low rank approximation.

See
1.24

The streaming entrywise low rank approximation algorithm of
[
JLL+21
]
roughly proceeds as follows. First, an oblivious sketch using
p
-stable random variables
is used to approximate the low rank approximation cost by the cost of an
n\times t
matrix for
t=k(\log n)^{O(1)}
. With only
t
columns, we can afford to approximate the
\ell_{p}
norm of the rows by the
\ell_{2}
norm of the rows, up to a
t^{1/p-1/2}
factor, using the equivalence of
\ell_{p}
norms. This is now just the subspace approximation problem, for which coreset constructions apply.

The following two lemmas show that sketching with
p
-stable variables preserves the objective function value. The first shows that the sketch does not contract objective function values for any column subset, while the second shows that the sketch does not expand objective function values by too much with fixed probability. The asymmetry in the sketching guarantees can be attributed to the heavy-tailedness of
p
-stable variables.

Lemma 9.1

(No Contraction, Lemma 2,
[
JLL+21
]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
and let
s\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\delta\in(0,\delta)
. Let
t=s(\log\frac{n}{\delta})^{O(1)}
, and let
\mathbf{G}\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times d}
be a matrix whose entries are i.i.d. standard
p
-stable random variables (see
[
JLL+21
, Definition 2.4]
), rescaled by
\Theta(1/t^{1/p})
. Then with probability at least
1-\delta
, for all subsets
T\subseteq[n]
with
\left\lvert T\right\rvert\leq s
and all
\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times T}
,

\left\lVert\mathbf{V}\mathbf{S}_{T}\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}\right\rVert_{p,p}\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{V}\mathbf{S}_{T}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\right\rVert_{p,p}

where
\mathbf{S}_{T}
is the sampling matrix associated with
T
.

Lemma 9.2

(No Expansion, Lemma E.17,
[
SWZ17
]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
. Let
\delta\in(0,\delta)
. Let
t\in\mathbb{N}
, and let
\mathbf{G}\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times d}
be a matrix whose entries are i.i.d. standard
p
-stable random variables, rescaled by
\Theta(1/t^{1/p})
. Then with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\right\rVert_{p,p}\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\left\lparen\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right\rparen^{O(1)}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\right\rVert_{p,p}.

We now return to the proof of Corollary
1.24
.

Proof of Corollary
1.24
.

Our online coreset algorithm is to first sketch with a
p
-stable matrix
\mathbf{G}\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times d}
, and then apply our Theorem
1.21
on
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}
. The
t
here is chosen as in Lemma
9.1
, with
s
being the sample complexity of our Theorem
1.21
.

Note that sketching with
\mathbf{G}
preserves the optimal rank
k
approximation cost in the
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{p,p}
norm by Lemmas
9.1
and
9.2
, which in turn preserves the optimal rank
k
approximation cost in
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{p,p}
norm, both up to a
\poly(n)
factor. Then, the rounding reduction to integer matrices as in Section
8.1
still works the same way, with sample complexity depending on the online condition number of
\mathbf{A}
rather than
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}
.

Let
\mathbf{S}
be the online coreset sampling matrix given by Theorem
1.21
with constant
\varepsilon
and
\delta
. Then, we have that

\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{X})\right\rVert_{p,2}=\Theta(1)\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{X})\right\rVert_{p,2}

for all rank
k
projections
\mathbf{X}
. Now let
\mathbf{X}^{*}
denote the optimal rank
k
projection for
\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}
. Note that
\mathbf{X}^{*}
can be written as
\mathbf{Y}^{*}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top})
, as it is a rank
k
projection in the row span of
\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}
. Then,

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}^{*}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\right\rVert_{p,2}\leq\Theta(1)\min_{\rank(\mathbf{X})\leq k}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{X})\right\rVert_{p,2}=\Theta(1)\min_{\rank(\mathbf{B})\leq k}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}-\mathbf{B}\right\rVert_{p,2}

so

\min_{\rank(\mathbf{V})\leq k}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}-\mathbf{V}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\right\rVert_{p,2}\leq\Theta(1)\min_{\rank(\mathbf{B})\leq k}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}-\mathbf{B}\right\rVert_{p,2}.

Let
\mathbf{V}^{*}
witness the minimum on the left hand side, and let
\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{k}=\argmin_{\rank(\tilde{\mathbf{A}})\leq k}\lVert\mathbf{A}-\tilde{\mathbf{A}}\rVert_{p,p}
. Then,

\displaystyle\min_{\rank(\mathbf{V})\leq k}\left\lVert\mathbf{V}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}\right\rVert_{p,p}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{V}^{*}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}\right\rVert_{p,p}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{V}^{*}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\right\rVert_{p,p}

No contraction (Lemma
9.1
)

\displaystyle\leq t^{1/p-1/2}\left\lVert\mathbf{V}^{*}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\right\rVert_{p,2}

Equivalence of
\ell_{p}
norms in
\mathbb{R}^{t}

\displaystyle\leq O(t^{1/p-1/2})\min_{\rank(\mathbf{B})\leq k}\left\lVert\mathbf{B}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\right\rVert_{p,2}

\displaystyle\leq O(t^{1/p-1/2})\min_{\rank(\mathbf{B})\leq k}\left\lVert\mathbf{B}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\right\rVert_{p,p}

Monotonicity of
\ell_{p}
norms

\displaystyle\leq O(t^{1/p-1/2})\left\lVert\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{k}\mathbf{G}^{\top}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\right\rVert_{p,p}

\displaystyle\leq O(t^{1/p-1/2})(\log n)^{O(1)}\left\lVert\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{k}-\mathbf{A}\right\rVert_{p,p}

\displaystyle\text{No expansion (Lemma \ref{lem:jllmw3})}\qed

10
Online Coresets for Euclidean
(k,p)
-Clustering

Our main result of this section is the following:

Theorem 10.1
.

Let
w^{\mathsf{OL}}
be a lower bound on all nonzero costs for
(k,p)
-clustering
\mathbf{A}_{i}
for
i\in[n]
, and let
W^{\mathsf{OL}}
similarly be an upper bound. Then, there is a strong online coreset algorithm which, with probability at least
1-\delta
, samples at most

\min\left\{\tilde{O}\left\lparen\varepsilon^{-4}k^{2}(\log n)^{3}\left\lparen\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right\rparen\log\frac{W^{\mathsf{OL}}}{w^{\mathsf{OL}}}\right\rparen,\tilde{O}\left\lparen\varepsilon^{-p-3}k(\log n)\left\lparen\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right\rparen^{2}\log\frac{W^{\mathsf{OL}}}{w^{\mathsf{OL}}}\right\rparen\right\}

points.

Proof.

The bulk of the work is in showing that we can get online sensitivity estimates
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}^{\mathsf{OL}}
which upper bound the true sensitivities and sum to at most

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}(\mathbf{A})\leq O\left\lparen k(\log n)^{2}\left\lparen\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right\rparen\log\frac{W^{\mathsf{OL}}}{w^{\mathsf{OL}}}\right\rparen

with probability at least
1-\delta
. We show this in Section
10.1
. The result then follows from a standard terminal embedding
[
NN19
]
and sensitivity sampling
[
FL11
]
argument (see Section 3 of
[
HV20
]
), done in an online manner (Theorem
B.9
). We defer the standard details to similar arguments in
[
HV20
]
.

We also adapt and improve another argument of
[
FL11
]
, which gives the latter sample complexity. The analysis is similar and is described in Section
10.2
.
∎

10.1
Online Sensitivity Approximation

The sensitivity approximation approach we take is to first compute a bicriteria solution, move the points to the bicriteria solution, and then compute the sensitivities of the resulting points, which is just the reciprocal of the cluster size.

We use the following result on online
k
clustering due to
[
LSS16
]
in order to obtain a bicriteria solution in an online manner. While
[
LSS16
]
only state their result for
k
-means clustering corresponding to
p=2
, we show that their algorithm generalizes straightforwardly to any
p\geq 1
in Appendix
C
.

Theorem 10.2

(
[
LSS16
]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
and
p\geq 1
. There is an online algorithm for Euclidean
(k,p)
-clustering, Algorithm
8
, which takes as input a cost lower bound
w^{*}
and immediately assigns each incoming point to at most

O\left\lparen k(\log n)\log\frac{W^{*}}{w^{*}}\right\rparen

clusters
\tilde{C}
and has cost at most

\sum_{i=1}^{n}d(\mathbf{a}_{i},\tilde{C})^{p}=O(W^{*}),

where

W^{*}=\min_{C\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d},\left\lvert C\right\rvert\leq k}\sum_{i=1}^{n}d(\mathbf{a}_{i},C)^{p}.

Algorithm 4

Online Sensitivity Approximation for Euclidean
(k,p)
-Clustering

input:

\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
, number of clusters
k
, cost lower bound
w^{*}
.

output:
Online sensitivity estimates
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}^{\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{A})
.

1:

C\leftarrow\varnothing

\triangleright
Bicriteria clustering using Algorithm
8

2:

v\leftarrow 0

3:

for

i\in[n]

do

4:

Update clustering
C
with
\mathbf{a}_{i}
using Algorithm
8

5:

\mathbf{c}_{i}\leftarrow\argmin_{\mathbf{c}\in C}\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}-\mathbf{c}\right\rVert_{2}^{p}

6:

v\leftarrow v+\left\lVert\mathbf{a}_{i}-\mathbf{c}\right\rVert_{2}^{p}

7:

S_{i}\leftarrow\left\{j\in[i]:\mathbf{c}_{j}=\mathbf{c}_{i}\right\}

\triangleright
The set of points in the same cluster as
i

8:

\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}^{\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{A})\leftarrow O(1)\left\lparen\frac{d(\mathbf{a}_{i},C)^{p}}{v}+\frac{1}{\left\lvert S_{i}\right\rvert}\right\rparen

The next lemma follows
[
HV20
, Lemma 5.5]
. While our result only works with constant probability for a fixed prefix stream
\mathbf{A}_{i}
, this can be boosted to
\poly(\delta/n)
probability by repetition and summing over the sensitivities, with only a
\log\frac{n}{\delta}
factor loss in the total sensitivity.

Lemma 10.3
.

Let
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}(\mathbf{A})
be output by Algorithm
4
(specifically Line
8
). Then, for each
i\in[n]
, with constant probability,

\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}(\mathbf{A})\geq\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}(\mathbf{A})=\sup_{C\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d},\left\lvert C\right\rvert\leq k}\frac{d(\mathbf{a}_{i},C)^{p}}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}d(\mathbf{a}_{j},C)^{p}}

Proof.

We condition on the success of the bicriteria solution at time
i
, which occurs with constant probability.

Let
C
be a set of
k
points. Then,

\displaystyle d(\mathbf{a}_{i},C)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}\left\lparen d(\mathbf{a}_{i},\mathbf{c}_{i})^{p}+d(\mathbf{c}_{i},C)^{p}\right\rparen

\displaystyle=2^{p-1}d(\mathbf{a}_{i},\mathbf{c}_{i})^{p}+2^{p-1}\frac{1}{\left\lvert S_{i}\right\rvert}\sum_{j\in S_{i}}d(\mathbf{c}_{j},C)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}d(\mathbf{a}_{i},\mathbf{c}_{i})^{p}+2^{p-1}\frac{1}{\left\lvert S_{i}\right\rvert}\sum_{j\in[n]}d(\mathbf{c}_{j},C)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}d(\mathbf{a}_{i},\mathbf{c}_{i})^{p}+2^{p-1}\cdot 2^{p-1}\frac{1}{\left\lvert S_{i}\right\rvert}\sum_{j\in[n]}d(\mathbf{c}_{j},\mathbf{a}_{j})^{p}+d(\mathbf{a}_{j},C)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\left\lparen d(\mathbf{a}_{i},\mathbf{c}_{i})^{p}+\frac{1}{\left\lvert S_{i}\right\rvert}\sum_{j\in[n]}d(\mathbf{a}_{j},C)^{p}\right\rparen

Factoring out
\sum_{j\in[n]}d(\mathbf{a}_{j},C)^{p}
yields the desired conclusion.
∎

Next, we bound the total sensitivity that is output by Algorithm
4
. Note that we can pass this guarantee to having good total sensitivity with probability
1
, but failing to upper bound the true sensitivities, by outputting zeros after the total sensitivity exceeds the desired threshold total.

Lemma 10.4
.

Let
w^{\mathsf{OL}}
be a lower bound on all nonzero costs for
(k,p)
-clustering
\mathbf{A}_{i}
for
i\in[n]
, and let
W^{\mathsf{OL}}
similarly be an upper bound. Then for each
i\in[n]
, with constant probability,

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}^{\mathsf{OL}}(\mathbf{A})\leq O\left\lparen k(\log n)^{2}\log\frac{W^{\mathsf{OL}}}{w^{\mathsf{OL}}}\right\rparen

Proof.

We condition on the success of the bicriteria solution at time
i
, which occurs with constant probability.

Note that the quantity
v
in Line
6
can double at most
O\left\lparen\log\left\lparen\frac{W^{\mathsf{OL}}}{w^{\mathsf{OL}}}\right\rparen\right\rparen
times. While
v
has not doubled, the quantity
d(\mathbf{a}_{i},C)^{p}/v
sums to at most
O(1)
. Thus, the total sensitivity contribution from this term is at most
O\left\lparen\log\left\lparen\frac{W^{\mathsf{OL}}}{w^{\mathsf{OL}}}\right\rparen\right\rparen
. To analyze the second term
1/\left\lvert S_{i}\right\rvert
, note that for a given one of the bicriteria clusters, these terms sum to at most
1+1/2+1/3+\dots+1/n=O(\log n)
. Then across all
O(k(\log n)\log\frac{W^{\mathsf{OL}}}{w^{\mathsf{OL}}})
clusters formed by the bicriteria solution, the total sensitivity contribution is
O(k(\log n)^{2}\log\frac{W^{\mathsf{OL}}}{w^{\mathsf{OL}}})
.
∎

10.2
Improved Feldman–Langberg Algorithm

Next, we show how to adapt an improved importance sampling algorithm of Feldman–Langberg
[
FL11
, Theorem 15.5]
. We show how to implement this algorithm in the online setting by using independent sampling without replacement in Theorem
B.17
, and along the way, streamline their argument, improve their dependence on
\varepsilon
by a
\varepsilon^{p-1}
factor, and generalize to the case when the initial bicriteria clustering algorithm outputs more than
k
centers.

Given Theorem
B.17
, our argument proceeds similarly to our first sensitivity sampling algorithm. For a constant probability of success of estimating the sampling score, we use the bicriteria algorithm of Theorem
10.2
, which outputs
O(k(\log n)\log\frac{W^{\mathsf{OL}}}{w^{\mathsf{OL}}})
centers. Then, the scores contributed by the reciprocal of the cluster size contributes
O(\frac{k}{\varepsilon^{2}}(\log\frac{k}{\delta})(\log n)^{2}\log\frac{W^{\mathsf{OL}}}{w^{\mathsf{OL}}})
. On the other hand, the scores contributed by the residual cost term can be analyzed by a similar cost doubling argument as before, which shows that they sum to
O(\log\frac{W^{\mathsf{OL}}}{w^{\mathsf{OL}}})
, so the sample complexity contribution from this term is
O((dk\log n+\log\frac{1}{\delta})\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{p+1}}\log\frac{W^{\mathsf{OL}}}{w^{\mathsf{OL}}})
. We can further use a terminal embedding to reduce
d
to
O(\varepsilon^{-2}\log n)
. Finally, we repeat the bicriteria algorithm
\log\frac{n}{\delta}
times to union bound over all
n
times
i\in[n]
, so that our final sample complexity is

O\left\lparen\frac{k}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\lparen\log\frac{k}{\delta}\right\rparen(\log n)^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{p+3}}k(\log n)\left\lparen\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right\rparen\right\rparen\left\lparen\log\frac{n}{\delta}\right\rparen\log\frac{W^{\mathsf{OL}}}{w^{\mathsf{OL}}}.

11
Lewis Weight Sampling for Active
\ell_{p}
Regression

We present our results for active
\ell_{p}
regression in the following sections.

We denote the optimal value of the
\ell_{p}
regression problem as

\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{b})\coloneqq\min_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}

and the optimal solution as

\mathbf{x}^{*}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{b})\coloneqq\arg\min_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}

We will often drop the dependence on
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{b}
and simply write
\mathsf{OPT}
and
\mathbf{x}^{*}
in many cases, if the design matrix and target vector are
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{b}
. However, when we need to consider different design matrices and target vectors, we will explicitly write the dependence on these objects.

11.1
Overview

Our algorithm will be to sample a Lewis weight sampling matrix
\mathbf{S}
(Definition
11.1
), sample the corresponding rows of
\mathbf{b}
, and the solve an
\ell_{p}
regression problem with design matrix
\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}
and target vector
\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}
. More formally, consider the following:

Definition 11.1

(Active
\ell_{p}
Regression Lewis Weight Sampling Matrix)
.

Let
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
and let
\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be
\alpha
-one-sided Lewis weights for
\mathbf{A}
. Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,
\gamma>0
, and
\delta\in(0,1)
. Then, we define the
active
\ell_{p}
regression Lewis weight sampling matrix

\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
to be a diagonal matrix with
\mathbf{S}_{i,i}
equal to
1/p_{i}
with probability
p_{i}
and
0
otherwise, for

\displaystyle p_{i}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\Theta(1)\frac{(p/2)^{\frac{p/2}{1-2/p}}}{\alpha^{p/2}}\frac{\mathbf{w}_{i}}{d\beta},1\right\}

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha\varepsilon^{p}}{\gamma\left\lVert\mathbf{w}\right\rVert_{1}^{p/2}\left[(\log(d\left\lVert\mathbf{w}\right\rVert_{1}))^{2}(\log n)+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right]}

Definition 11.2
.

Let
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
be a sampling matrix. Then, let

\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}\coloneqq\mathbf{x}^{*}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A},\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b})=\arg\min_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}.

Our main result is the following theorem, which shows that solving the Lewis weight-sampled system yields
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximate solutions, whenever solving
\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}
gives a constant factor solution.

Theorem 11.3
.

Let
\mathbf{S}
be a sampling matrix as defined in Definition
11.1
with
\gamma=O(\varepsilon/\poly\log(1/\varepsilon))
. Then, with probability at least
1-O(\log\log(1/\varepsilon))\cdot\delta
, the following implication is true:

\displaystyle\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A},\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b})=O(\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{b}))\implies\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}\leq(1+\varepsilon)\mathsf{OPT}.

We will first use Theorem
11.3
to design a high-probability algorithm for solving active
\ell_{p}
linear regression in Section
11.2
, and then prove Theorem
11.3
in the rest of the section.

11.2
Algorithm for Active
\ell_{p}
Regression

Given Theorem
11.3
, all we need to do is to identify a constant factor solution, and this will be a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximate solution with high probability.
[
MMWY22
, Algorithm 5]
provides such an algorithm, in which a “median”-like procedure is used to find a good candidate among
O(\log\frac{1}{\delta})
constant-probability candidates, reproduced in Algorithm
5
below. Our approach is to combine this algorithm with the analysis of Theorem
11.3
.

Algorithm 5

Probability Boosting for Constant Factor Active
\ell_{p}
Regression

input:

\ell
candidate solutions
\mathbf{x}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{x}_{\ell}
with at least
9/10\cdot\ell
satisfying
\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{i}-\mathbf{b}\rVert_{p}\leq\alpha\min_{\mathbf{x}}\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{b}\rVert_{p}
.

output:
Approximate solution
\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
to
\min_{\mathbf{x}}\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{b}\rVert_{p}
.

1:

Let
\mathbf{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell^{2}}
contain all pairwise distances
\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{i}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{j}\rVert_{p}
(over ordered pairs
(i,j)
) sorted in increasing order. Let
\tau=\mathbf{d}(\left\lfloor\ell^{2}\cdot 8/10\right\rfloor)
be the
80^{th}
percentile distance.

2:

Return any
\mathbf{x}_{i}
such that
\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{i}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{j}\rVert_{p}\leq\tau
for at least
1/2\cdot\ell
vectors
\mathbf{x}_{j}
.

Algorithm 6

High Probability Active
\ell_{p}
Regression

input:

\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
,
\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
\varepsilon>0
,
\delta\in(0,1)
,
p>2
.

output:
Approximate solution
\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
to
\min_{\mathbf{x}}\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{b}\rVert_{p}
.

1:

Compute one-sided lewis weights
\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
\left\lVert\mathbf{w}\right\rVert_{1}=O(d)
(see, e.g.,
[
JLS22
]
)

2:

For each
i\in[\ell]
for
\ell=O(\log\frac{1}{\delta})
, let
\mathbf{S}^{i}
be a Lewis weight sample generated as in Definition
11.1

3:

For each
i\in[\ell]
, let
\mathbf{x}_{i}=\arg\min_{\mathbf{x}}\left\lVert\mathbf{S}^{i}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{S}^{i}\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}

4:

Run Algorithm
5
on the
\ell
candidates and output the solution

Theorem 11.4

(Active
\ell_{p}
Regression)
.

Let
\tilde{\mathbf{x}}
be the output of Algorithm
6
with failure rate in the sampling process of Definition
11.1
set to
\delta/\ell\log\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}
and
\gamma
set to
\varepsilon\poly\log(1/\varepsilon)
. Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
,
\tilde{\mathbf{x}}
satisfies

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\tilde{\mathbf{x}}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}\leq(1+\varepsilon)\mathsf{OPT}.

Furthermore, Algorithm
6
reads at most

O\left\lparen\frac{d^{p/2}}{\varepsilon^{p-1}}\left[(\log d)^{2}(\log n)+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right]\poly\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right\rparen

entries of
\mathbf{b}
.

Proof.

We first apply Theorem
11.3
with failure probability
\delta/\ell\log\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}
and union bound over all
\ell
trials in Line
2
, so that with probability at least
1-\delta
, the conclusion of Theorem
11.3
holds for all
\ell
trials simultaneously.

By
[
MMWY22
, Theorem 3.2]
, Line
3
of Algorithm
6
yields a constant factor solution with probability at least
0.99
. Then with probability at least
1-\delta
, at least a
0.9
fraction of the
\ell
solutions obtained in Line
3
are constant factor solutions. Then by the proof of
[
MMWY22
, Theorem 3.3]
, Line
4
returns a constant factor solution, and thus a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximate solution.
∎

By using
online
Lewis weights
[
WY23
]
rather than the usual Lewis weights in Theorem
11.4
, we obtain the first
\ell_{p}
online active regression algorithm for
p>2
:

Corollary 11.5

(Online Active
\ell_{p}
Regression)
.

Let
\tilde{\mathbf{x}}
be the output of Algorithm
6
with failure rate in the sampling process of Definition
11.1
set to
\delta/\ell\log\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}
and
\gamma
set to
\varepsilon\poly\log(1/\varepsilon)
, and Lewis weights in Line
1
replaced by online Lewis weights. Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
,
\tilde{\mathbf{x}}
satisfies

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\tilde{\mathbf{x}}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}\leq(1+\varepsilon)\mathsf{OPT}.

Furthermore, Algorithm
6
reads at most

O\left\lparen\frac{d^{p/2}}{\varepsilon^{p-1}}(\log(n\kappa^{\mathsf{OL}}))^{p/2+1}\left[(\log d)^{2}(\log n)+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right]\poly\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right\rparen

entries of
\mathbf{b}
, in an online manner, where
\kappa^{\mathsf{OL}}
is the
online condition number
of
\mathbf{A}
.

11.3
Closeness of Near-Optimal Solutions

We start by showing that a
(1+\gamma)
-approximate solution must be
O(\gamma^{1/p})\mathsf{OPT}
-close to the optimal solution, similar to Theorem 3.19 of
[
MMWY22
]
. In
[
MMWY22
]
, a similar result is shown for
1<p<2
using the strong convexity of
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{p}^{2}
. For
p>2
, strong convexity unfortunately does not hold. Nonetheless, we still obtain a similar statement by using a bound on the Bregman divergence of
\ell_{p}
norms shown by
[
AKPS19
]
.

Lemma 11.6

(Bregman Divergence of
\ell_{p}
Norms
[
AKPS19
]
)
.

Let
p\geq 2
. Then, for
\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we have

\left\lVert\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}\geq\left\lVert\mathbf{y}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}-p\left\langle\mathbf{y}^{p-1},\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\right\rangle+\frac{p-1}{p2^{p}}\left\lVert\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{p}^{p},

where
\mathbf{y}^{p-1}
denotes the signed entrywise
(p-1)
th power, i.e.,
\mathbf{y}_{i}^{p-1}=\sgn(\mathbf{y}_{i})\left\lvert\mathbf{y}_{i}\right\rvert^{p-1}
.

Proof.

We set
\Delta=\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{y}
and apply
[
AKPS19
, Lemma 4.6]
to bound the Bregman divergence by the
\gamma_{p}
function, which is turn lower bounded by the
\ell_{p}
norm by
[
AKPS19
, Lemma 3.2]
for
p\geq 2
.
∎

The following is an immediate consequence:

Lemma 11.7
.

Let
p\geq 2
. Let
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
and
\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then, for any
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}\leq(1+\gamma)\mathsf{OPT}

with
\gamma\in(0,1/p)
, we have that

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}\right\rVert_{p}\leq O(\gamma^{1/p})\mathsf{OPT},

where
\mathbf{x}^{*}\coloneqq\arg\min_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}
.

Proof.

The KKT conditions require that

\left\langle(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{b})^{p-1},\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}\right\rangle=0

for all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We then apply Lemma
11.6
with
\mathbf{y}^{\prime}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{b}
and
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{b}
to conclude that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\geq\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}-\left\langle(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{b})^{p-1},\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}\right\rangle+\frac{p-1}{p2^{p}}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}+\frac{p-1}{p2^{p}}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}.

Then,

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}\right\rVert_{p}\leq\left\lparen\frac{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}}{(p-1)/p2^{p}}\right\rparen^{1/p}\leq O(\gamma^{1/p})\mathsf{OPT}.\qed

Lemma
11.7
implies that a
(1+\gamma)
-approximate solution can never output a solution that is more than
O(\gamma^{1/p})\mathsf{OPT}
from the optimum, in the column space of
\mathbf{A}
equipped with
\ell_{p}
. In turn, this means that it suffices to bound the distortion of the Lewis weight sampling process over a ball of radius
O(\gamma^{1/p})\mathsf{OPT}
.

11.4
Lewis Weight Sampling for Near-Optimal Solutions

As done in
[
MMWY22
]
, we seek to bound the distortion in the difference between the cost of
\mathbf{x}
and
\mathbf{x}^{*}
, since this suffices to find a near-optimal
\mathbf{x}
. That is, we first define the cost difference

\Delta_{i}(\mathbf{x})\coloneqq\left\lvert[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{b}](i)\right\rvert^{p}-\left\lvert[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{b}](i)\right\rvert^{p}

for each
i\in[n]
and
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We then seek a bound on

\left\lvert\left[\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}\right]-\left[\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}\right]\right\rvert=\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{S}_{i,i}-1)\Delta_{i}(\mathbf{x})\right\rvert

for
\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}
. The following lemma shows why:

Lemma 11.8
.

Suppose that

\left\lvert\left[\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}\right]-\left[\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}\right]\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon\mathsf{OPT}^{p}.

Then,

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}+\varepsilon\mathsf{OPT}^{p}.

Proof.

We have that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}+\left\lparen\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}\right\rparen

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}+\left\lparen\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}\right\rparen-\left\lparen\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}\right\rparen

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+\left\lparen\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}\right\rparen

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}+\left\lvert\left\lparen\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}\right\rparen-\left\lparen\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}\right\rparen\right\rvert

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+\left\lparen\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}\right\rparen

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}+\varepsilon\mathsf{OPT}^{p}+\left\lparen\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}-\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}\right\rparen

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}+\varepsilon\mathsf{OPT}^{p}

as claimed.
∎

In Section
12
, we show the following moment bound on the quality of uniform sampling on a matrix with uniformly bounded Lewis weights.

Theorem 11.9
.

Let
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
and let
\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be
\alpha
-one-sided Lewis weights for
\mathbf{A}
such that
\mathbf{w}_{i}\leq Wd/n
for each
i\in[n]
. Let
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
be a diagonal matrix with
\mathbf{S}_{i,i}=1+\sigma_{i}
for independent Rademacher variables
\sigma_{i}\in\{\pm 1\}
. Let
\gamma>0
. Then, for all
l\geq 1
,

\E\sup_{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}\right\rVert_{p}\leq\gamma^{1/p}\mathsf{OPT}}\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}\Delta_{i}(\mathbf{x})\right\rvert^{l}\leq\left\lparen O(\varepsilon)\mathsf{OPT}^{p}\right\rparen^{l}

(17)

where

\varepsilon=O(1)\left[\gamma\frac{W\left\lVert\mathbf{w}\right\rVert_{1}^{p/2}}{\alpha n}\left[((\log(d\left\lVert\mathbf{w}\right\rVert_{1})^{2}(\log n))^{1+1/l}+l\right]\right]^{1/p}.

Following a symmetrization argument of
[
WY23
, Theorem 5.2]
, this gives the following guarantee on Lewis weight sampling:

Theorem 11.10
.

Let
\mathbf{S}
be a sampling matrix as defined in Definition
11.1
, where
\gamma<\gamma_{0}
for a sufficiently small constant
\gamma_{0}
. Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
, the following implication is true:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}\leq(1+\gamma)\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{b})\implies

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\sup_{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}\right\rVert_{p}\leq\gamma^{1/p}\mathsf{OPT}}\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{S}_{i,i}-1)\Delta_{i}\right\rvert\leq O(\varepsilon)(\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{b})+\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A},\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b})).

Proof.

The proof closely follows
[
WY23
, Theorem 5.2]
, and we defer many of the details to their proof. In order to apply the result of Theorem
11.9
to this setting, we will bound

\E_{\mathbf{S}}\sup_{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}\right\rVert_{p}\leq\gamma^{1/p}\mathsf{OPT}}\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{S}_{i,i}-1)\Delta_{i}(\mathbf{x})\right\rvert^{l}

(18)

for
l=O(\log\frac{1}{\delta}+\log\log n)
. To bound this moment,
[
WY23
, Theorem 5.2]
shows a symmetrization argument which allows one to bound (
18
) by

2^{l}\cdot\E_{\sigma}\sup_{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}\right\rVert_{p}\leq\gamma^{1/p}\mathsf{OPT}}\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}\Delta_{i}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})\right\rvert^{l},

where
\Delta_{i}^{\prime}
is the analogue of
\Delta_{i}
defined with the matrix
\mathbf{A}
and label vector
\mathbf{b}
replaced by a different matrix and vector
\mathbf{A}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{b}^{\prime}
, and weights
\mathbf{w}
replaced by a different set of
O(\alpha)
-one-sided weights
\mathbf{w}^{\prime}
. Here,
\mathbf{A}^{\prime}
is obtained by concatenating a “flattened” version of
\mathbf{A}
together with
\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}
, and the weights
\mathbf{w}^{\prime}
are obtained as a “batch online” extension of
\mathbf{w}
to
\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}
. The label vector
\mathbf{b}
can similarly be flattened and sampled to obtain
\mathbf{b}^{\prime}
. Note then that
\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime},\mathbf{b}^{\prime})\leq O(\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{b})+\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A},\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b}))
. Furthermore,
\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}
is an
O(1)

\ell_{p}
subspace embedding for
\mathbf{A}
with probability at least
1-\delta
by
[
WY23
, Theorem 5.2]
, so the new matrix and weights have the property that
\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{p}=\Theta(1)\left\lVert\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{p}
for all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. It also follows from the proof of
[
WY23
, Theorem 5.2]
that
\left\lVert\mathbf{w}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq d\beta
.

Furthermore, suppose that
\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}\leq(1+\gamma)\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{b})
. First note that
\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime},\mathbf{b}^{\prime})\geq\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{b})+\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A},\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b})
since separately optimizing the two parts of the concatenation can only decrease the cost. Then,
\mathbf{x}^{*}_{\mathbf{S}}
is a
(1+\gamma)
-approximate solution for
(\mathbf{A}^{\prime},\mathbf{b}^{\prime})
, since the cost on
(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{b})
is at most
(1+\gamma)\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{b})
and the cost on
(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A},\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b})
is
\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A},\mathbf{S}\mathbf{b})
. Then if
\mathbf{x}^{*}_{\mathrm{concat}}
is the optimal solution for
(\mathbf{A}^{\prime},\mathbf{b}^{\prime})
, then by Lemma
11.7
and using that
\gamma<\gamma_{0}
for a small enough
\gamma_{0}
, we have that
\left\lVert\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{concat}}^{*}\right\rVert_{p}\leq O(\gamma^{1/p})\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime},\mathbf{b}^{\prime})
. Similarly,
\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}\right\rVert_{p}\leq O(\gamma^{1/p})\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{b})
. Then for any
\mathbf{x}
such that
\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}\right\rVert_{p}\leq\gamma^{1/p}\mathsf{OPT}
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}_{\mathrm{concat}}\right\rVert_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}\right\rVert_{p}+\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}_{\mathbf{S}}\right\rVert_{p}+\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{concat}}^{*}\right\rVert_{p}

triangle inequality

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{1/p}\mathsf{OPT}+O(\gamma^{1/p})\mathsf{OPT}+O(\gamma^{1/p})\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime},\mathbf{b}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=O(\gamma^{1/p})\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime},\mathbf{b}^{\prime})

and similarly
\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}_{\mathrm{concat}}\right\rVert_{p}\leq O(\gamma^{1/p})\mathsf{OPT}
by using that
\mathbf{S}
is a subspace embedding. We may thus bound

\E_{\sigma}\sup_{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}^{*}\right\rVert_{p}\leq\gamma^{1/p}\mathsf{OPT}}\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}\Delta_{i}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})\right\rvert^{l}\leq\E_{\sigma}\sup_{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{concat}}^{*}\right\rVert_{p}\leq O(\gamma^{1/p})\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime},\mathbf{b}^{\prime})}\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}\Delta_{i}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})\right\rvert^{l}.

Theorem
11.9
now applies on this new matrix and weights, and we bound (
18
) by
(O(\varepsilon)\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime},\mathbf{b}^{\prime}))^{l}
. An application of Markov’s inequality and taking
l
th roots yields our claim.
∎

11.5
Proof of Theorem
11.3

We are now in a position to prove Theorem
11.3
.

Proof of Theorem
11.3
.

Let
C
be a sufficiently large constant to be chosen later.

Define
\beta_{i}
recursively by
\beta_{1}=(p-1)/p
and
\beta_{i+1}=(\beta_{i}+p-1)/p
, which has the closed form solution

\beta_{i}=1-p^{-i}.

Then for
i\in[I]
for
I=O(\log\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon})
, we apply Theorem
11.10
with
\varepsilon
and
\gamma
in the theorem set to
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\varepsilon^{\beta_{i+1}}
and
\gamma
in the theorem set to
\gamma^{\prime}=C^{i}\varepsilon^{\beta_{i}}
. Note that for every
i
, we have

\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{\varepsilon^{\prime p}}=O(C^{i})\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta_{i}}}{\varepsilon^{\beta_{i+1}p}}=O(C^{i})\varepsilon^{\beta_{i}-\beta_{i}-(p-1)}=O(C^{i})\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{p-1}}.

Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{S}}^{*}-\mathbf{b}\right\rVert_{p}^{p}\leq(1+C^{i}\varepsilon^{\beta_{i}})\mathsf{OPT}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{b})^{p}\implies
